They do still matter to me, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like I suddenly don’t care about them at all. It’s more that when I think about starting—like getting my camera bag ready or reaching out about mentoring—it feels… heavier than it should.

A little, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m falling apart or anything—work is still getting done overall—but I’ve noticed I’m not as sharp with some of the details.

And outside of work, I think part of why I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring is that I don’t feel as present as I should. I don’t want to show up halfway, if that makes sense. Does that answer what you mean?

Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because it’s not like I hate those things now or anything.

Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I’ll feel like I’m the only one who can straighten out the doctors, the family, and the office, and I start believing my system is better than anyone else’s judgment.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: There have been nights when I sleep two or three hours, wake up before dawn, and feel ready to answer emails or update appointment lists instead of feeling tired.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: My wife and coworkers tell me I talk so fast during those periods that I jump from medical forms to work deadlines to mentoring ideas before they can get a word in.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my high-energy stretches, the certainty that messages or calendar problems meant something special would get stronger, and I would sometimes hear those short comments while I was running on very little sleep.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this whole period, I’ve been cycling back I don’t want to make it sound like I suddenly don’t care about them at all. It’s more that when I think about starting—like getting my camera bag ready or reaching out about mentoring—it feels… heavier than it should.\n\nOnce I’m there, sometimes I can get into it a little, but the pull toward it isn’t the same. Those things used to feel like they gave me energy, like they were part of who I am. Lately they feel kind of flat, and I’ve just stopped being the person who initiates them.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s enjoyment being gone, or motivation, or what you’d call it. That bothers me, because being reliable is kind of… my thing.\n\nAnd outside of work, I think part of why I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring is that I don’t feel as present as I should. I don’t want to show up halfway, if that makes sense. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, even when you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because it’s not like I hate those things now or anything.\n\nBut the things that usually make me feel like myself—photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean and organized—they’ve started feeling kind of flat. I’d think about doing them, and then… I wouldn’t. That’s not really like me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, no. I still care about them, and I think that’s part of why it bothers me.

It’s more like… the things that usually make me feel like myself—going out with my camera, mentoring the kids, even just keeping my calendar clean—started feeling flat and heavy. And then I just stopped being the one to start them.

So I guess it’s not that they mean nothing to me. It’s more that I can’t seem to get myself to do them the way I used to. Does that make sense?

It’s more that when I think about starting—like getting my camera bag ready or reaching out about mentoring—it feels… heavier than it should.\n\nOnce I’m there, sometimes I can get into it a little, but the pull toward it isn’t the same. Those things used to feel like they gave me energy, like they were part of who I am. Lately they feel kind of flat, and I’ve just stopped being the person who initiates them.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s enjoyment being gone, or motivation, or what you’d call it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus, make decisions, or think clearly lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m falling apart or anything—work is still getting done overall—but I’ve noticed I’m not as sharp with some of the details.\n\nLike with calendars, I’ve missed a couple of things I normally wouldn’t miss, or I’ll catch myself having to double-check tasks that used to feel automatic. That bothers me, because being reliable is kind of… my thing.\n\nAnd outside of work, I think part of why I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring is that I don’t feel as present as I should. I don’t want to show up halfway, if that makes sense. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, even when you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because it’s not like I hate those things now or anything.\n\nBut the things that usually make me feel like myself—photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean and organized—they’ve started feeling kind of flat. I’d think about doing them, and then… I wouldn’t. That’s not really like me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in those activities, or is it more that you still care about them but just can’t seem to get yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, no. I still care about them, and I think that’s part of why it bothers me.\n\nIt’s more like… the things that usually make me feel like myself—going out with my camera, mentoring the kids, even just keeping my calendar clean—started feeling flat and heavy. Um… I guess what brought me in is that some of the things that usually keep me feeling like myself have started to feel kind of… flat. Not terrible, exactly, but heavier than they should.\n\nI used to like going on photography walks, helping mentor the kids, even just keeping my calendar clean and organized. Lately I’ve sort of stopped starting those things, if that makes sense. I’m not trying to be lazy or anything—I just don’t seem to get myself going the way I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things as much as you used to, or that activities you once looked forward to feel more like a chore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing things, but the stuff that usually helps me feel like me—going out for photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar neat—has started to feel kind of flat. Like heavier than it should.\n\nI still know those things matter to me, but I’m not really starting them the way I used to. They feel more like something I have to push myself toward, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I’d like to admit, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing things, but the stuff that usually helps me feel like me—going out for photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar neat—has started to feel kind of flat. Like heavier than it should.\n\nI still know those things matter to me, but I’m not really starting them the way I used to. They feel more like something I have to push myself toward, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I’d like to admit, I guess. Maybe about a year and three months.\n\nIt kind of started after a family member’s health and behavior changed, and I ended up taking on more of the coordinating and keeping-track-of-things. I don’t want to overstate it, but since then, this flat feeling has just sort of stayed around. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days you feel down or sad, and if so, how long does that feeling usually last?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it “sad” most days, exactly. It’s more like… low or kind of dulled out. Some days it’s there in the background for a good chunk of the day, but I can still get through what I need to do.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything… just not as light as I used to feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel fatigued or that you’ve lost your energy, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I’m not sure if I’d call it physical fatigue exactly. It’s more like I have to push harder to start things.\n\nThe reason I came in is that the things that usually make me feel like myself—my photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean—started feeling kind of flat and heavy. And I just… stopped initiating them the way I normally would.\n\nI can still do things when I really need to, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but the energy to begin is definitely not there like it used to be. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say “depressed most of the day nearly every day,” no. At least… that sounds stronger than what I’ve been feeling.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes, or sits in the background. Some days are heavier, but I can still function and be pleasant with people. I’m not sure if I’m answering that in the way you’re looking for, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you’ve felt when you’ve tried to start those activities, like your photography walks or mentoring the kids—do they still feel meaningful or enjoyable to you, even if you have to push yourself to start them?\n\nPatient: They do still matter to me, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like I suddenly don’t care about them at all. It’s more that when I think about starting—like getting my camera bag ready or reaching out about mentoring—it feels… heavier than it should.\n\nOnce I’m there, sometimes I can get into it a little, but the pull toward it isn’t the same. Those things used to feel like they gave me energy, like they were part of who I am. Lately they feel kind of flat, and I’ve just stopped being the person who initiates them.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s enjoyment being gone, or motivation, or what you’d call it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus, make decisions, or think clearly lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m falling apart or anything—work is still getting done overall—but I’ve noticed I’m not as sharp with some of the details.\n\nLike with calendars, I’ve missed a couple of things I normally wouldn’t miss, or I’ll catch myself having to double-check tasks that used to feel automatic. That bothers me, because being reliable is kind of… my thing.\n\nAnd outside of work, I think part of why I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring is that I don’t feel as present as I should. I don’t want to show up halfway, if that makes sense. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, even when you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because it’s not like I hate those things now or anything.\n\nBut the things that usually make me feel like myself—photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean and organized—they’ve started feeling kind of flat. Heavy, maybe. And even when I do manage to start, it doesn’t quite give me the same feeling it used to.\n\nHonestly, what made me come in is that I just stopped initiating them. I’d think about doing them, and then… I wouldn’t. That’s not really like me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in those activities, or is it more that you still care about them but just can’t seem to get yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, no. I still care about them, and I think that’s part of why it bothers me.\n\nIt’s more like… the things that usually make me feel like myself—going out with my camera, mentoring the kids, even just keeping my calendar clean—started feeling flat and heavy. And then I just stopped being the one to start them.\n\nSo I guess it’s not that they mean nothing to me. It’s more that I can’t seem to get myself to do them the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you do manage to start those activities, like the photography walks or mentoring, you get less pleasure or satisfaction out of them than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is less satisfying than it used to be. Not gone completely, I don’t want to overstate it, but it feels flatter.\n\nLike, if I do go out with my camera, I can still recognize that it’s something I care about, but it doesn’t really lift me the same way. Same with mentoring—I’m glad it matters, but I don’t feel as engaged or energized by it.\n\nWhat made me come in is that those things usually make me feel like myself, and lately they’ve felt kind of heavy, so I’ve stopped initiating them. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your ability to get things done at work or at home has slowed down or become more difficult because of these feelings?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still getting my work done overall—I don’t want to make it sound like everything’s falling apart.\n\nBut I have noticed I’m missing small calendar details, or I’ll have to double-check things that used to be automatic for me. That bothers me, because being on top of those things is kind of my job.\n\nAt home, or outside of work, it’s more that I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring. I don’t feel as present or reliable as I usually do, so I think I’ve avoided starting them. I’m not sure if that counts as impairment, but it has made things harder.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or down for most of the day, more days than not, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say it’s been persistently low most of the day, exactly. I mean… I’ve had down stretches, sure, but I don’t want to label it bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s more like feeling kind of muted or weighed down at times, especially when I’m trying to get started on things. But “most of the day, more days than not” — I’d have to think about that. I don’t know if I can say yes to that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, would you say that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say nearly every day, no. I’ve definitely had moments where I feel down or kind of heavy, but “most of the day” feels stronger than what I’m noticing.\n\nI don’t want to underreport it either, but… if you’re asking strictly, I’d say I’m not sure it reaches that level. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I’m not sure if that counts as impairment, but it has made things harder.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or down for most of the day, more days than not, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say it’s been persistently low most of the day, exactly. I mean… I’ve had down stretches, sure, but I don’t want to label it bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s more like feeling kind of muted or weighed down at times, especially when I’m trying to get started on things. But “most of the day, more days than not” — I’d have to think about that. I don’t know if I can say yes to that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, would you say that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say nearly every day, no. I’ve definitely had moments where I feel down or kind of heavy, but “most of the day” feels stronger than what I’m noticing.\n\nI don’t want to underreport it either, but… if you’re asking strictly, I’d say I’m not sure it reaches that level. Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nOver the past two weeks, have you had days where your mood felt down or low for most of the day?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 46 / executive_secretary_or_executive_administrative_assistant / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Maybe a few, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say “most of the day,” because I don’t know that I’ve been tracking it that clearly.

There have been days where I felt lower or heavier for a good chunk of the day, but not like I was completely unable to function. I’m still doing what I need to do, mostly. So… yes, some days, but I’m not sure how strong to make that answer.

Yeah… I think that’s actually part of why I came in.

I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I still care about those things in my head, if that makes sense. I don’t want to show up halfway, if that makes sense. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, even when you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because it’s not like I hate those things now or anything.\n\nBut the things that usually make me feel like myself—photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean and organized—they’ve started feeling kind of flat. Heavy, maybe. And even when I do manage to start, it doesn’t quite give me the same feeling it used to.\n\nHonestly, what made me come in is that I just stopped initiating them. I’d think about doing them, and then… I wouldn’t. That’s not really like me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in those activities, or is it more that you still care about them but just can’t seem to get yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, no.
